```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))
        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d > dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

Let's maybe examine 0-1 BFS invariant and stale check more deeply to ensure no hidden bug with `if d > dist[i][j]: continue` and early return.

Standard 0-1 BFS:
```
dist[s]=0
deque q
q.append(s)
while q:
    v = q.popleft()
    for u,w:
        if dist[v] + w < dist[u]:
            dist[u] = dist[v] + w
            if w == 0: q.appendleft(u)
            else: q.append(u)
```
Some implementations don't store distance in queue, only node, because dist[v] is current. But if duplicate nodes in queue, when popped, dist[v] may have been updated to smaller, so using dist[v] is correct. Our queue stores d. If we don't skip stale, using d could be larger than dist[v], causing wrong relaxations. So stale check is necessary when storing d. If we stored only node, no stale issue because dist[v] is current. But we store d. So include stale check.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
